A quoted field containing a comma is read as one value (RFC 4180 via `encoding/csv`). |\n| INPUT-002 | Trim header names and values | Leading and trailing spaces are removed from every header name and value before null, type, distinct, and output rules apply. |\n| INPUT-003 | Reject empty files, malformed headers, and ragged records | An empty file, an empty or repeated header name, or a record whose field count differs from the header exits 3 with nothing on stdout and one stderr line naming the file and line number. |\n\n### Feature: Profile Counts\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| COUNTS-001 | Report rows, nulls, and distinct counts per column | `rows` counts data records. `nulls` counts values that are empty or equal `NA`, `N/A`, or `NULL` ignoring case. `distinct` counts distinct non-null values compared as exact trimmed strings. |\n\n### Feature: Column Types\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| TYPES-001 | Infer integer and number column types | `integer` when every non-null value matches `-?[0-9]+`. `number` when every non-null value matches `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?` and the column is not integer. |\n| TYPES-002 | Infer date column types and reject invalid calendar dates | `date` when every non-null value matches `YYYY-MM-DD` and is a real calendar date. A value such as `2025-02-30` makes the column not date. |\n| TYPES-003 | Infer boolean, text, and empty column types | `boolean` when every non-null value equals `true` or `false` ignoring case. `text` when no earlier type fits. `empty` when the column has no non-null value. |\n\n### Feature: Column Statistics\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| STATS-001 | Report min and max for every column type | `integer` as integers. `number` as two-decimal values. `date` as earliest and latest. `boolean` as `false` if any value is false, else `true`, and `true` if any value is true, else `false`. `text` as smallest and largest by byte value. `empty` blank. |\n| STATS-002 | Report sum and mean for integer and number columns | `sum` is the total of non-null values. `mean` is the total divided by the non-null count. Other types leave both blank. |\n| STATS-003 | Report median for integer and number columns | `median` is the middle sorted non-null value, or the mean of the two middle values when the count is even. Other types leave it blank. |\n| STATS-004 | Write numeric statistics with two decimal places | Every `number` min, max, sum, mean, and median, and every integer mean and median, is written with exactly two decimals, rounded half away from zero, with a leading `-` when negative. Integer min, max, and sum are plain integers. |\n\n### Feature: Column Selection\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| SELECT-001 | Profile one named column with `--column` | Output is the profile header and one row for the named column only. |\n| SELECT-002 | Reject a named column that is absent from the header | `--column` or `--freq` naming a column not in the header exits 3 with nothing on stdout. |\n\n### Feature: Frequency Listing\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| FREQ-001 | List the most frequent values with `--freq` | Header `value,count`, then distinct non-null values ordered by count descending, then value ascending by byte value. Null values are not listed. An all-null column writes only the header. |\n| FREQ-002 | Limit frequency output with `--top` | `--top` defaults to 5 and accepts a positive integer only. `--top` without `--freq` is a usage error. |\n\n### Feature: Command Line Interface\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| CLI-001 | Accept `csvstat [--column <name>] <file>` and `csvstat --freq <name> [--top <n>] <file>` | Options precede exactly one file. `go build ./cmd/csvstat` produces the executable using only the standard library. |\n| CLI-002 | Map usage errors to exit code 2 | A missing or extra file, an unknown option, a missing option value, an invalid `--top`, `--top` without `--freq`, or `--column` with `--freq` exits 2 with nothing on stdout. |\n| CLI-003 | Map an unreadable file to exit code 1 | A file that cannot be opened or read exits 1 with nothing on stdout. |\n\n### Feature: Verification\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| QA-001 | Provide a POSIX `bin/test.sh` | `bin/test.sh` is POSIX sh, passes `sh -n`, runs `go vet ./...` and `go test ./...` from the application root, and exits zero only when both pass. |\n| QA-002 | Pass the supplied grader end to end | `sh sources/run.sh` exits zero against the completed application, and every supplied check passes with zero failures. |\n\n## Surfaced Acceptance Criteria\n\n| ID | Story ID | Criterion |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC-001 | CLI-001 | `csvstat --help` writes usage text naming both command forms and every option to standard output and exits 0. |\n| AC-002 | SELECT-001 | The `--column` and `--freq` name arguments match a trimmed header name exactly and case-sensitively. |\n| AC-003 | STATS-002 | Integer sum and mean are computed with arbitrary precision so that no digit string overflows. |\n| AC-004 | STATS-004 | Number sums, means, and medians are computed from exact decimal values, not binary floating point, and rounded half away from zero only at output. |\n| AC-005 | INPUT-003 | A CSV syntax error reported by `encoding/csv`, such as a bare quote in an unquoted field, exits 3 with nothing on stdout and a stderr line naming the file and line number. |\n| AC-006 | CLI-003 | A path that is a directory, or any path that cannot be opened or read, exits 1 with nothing on stdout. |\n| AC-007 | INPUT-001 | Input bytes are compared as bytes. A byte-order mark is not stripped, and the file is not rejected for invalid UTF-8. |\n\n## Relationship Model\n\n| Source or group | Relationship type | Related source or group | Evidence | Delivery implication |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md | instruction-to-test | sources/run.sh | \"Run Harness\" section names the 22-check grader and its build command. | The grader must be staged verbatim, and the executable must build at `./cmd/csvstat`. |\n| sources/run.sh | test-kit-to-implementation | sources/employees.csv, sources/sensors.csv, sources/orders.csv, sources/header_only.csv, sources/ragged.csv | `DATA` resolves to the directory holding these files (checks A1–A4, E1–E3). | All five fixtures must be staged beside `run.sh` under `sources/`. |\n| sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md | instruction-to-test | sources/employees.csv, sources/sensors.csv, sources/orders.csv, sources/header_only.csv, sources/ragged.csv | \"Read-only scoring assets\" lists each fixture and its shape. | Fixtures are read-only; no story may rewrite them. |\n| sources/run.sh | test-kit-to-implementation | `bin/test.sh` | INSTRUCTIONS \"Tests\" requires `bin/test.sh` to run `go vet` and `go test`. | QA-001 must provide the test runner that the terminal story depends on. |\n\n## Source Roles\n\n| Path | Role | Plan disposition | Build disposition |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md | normative specification | context | prompt-only |\n| sources/run.sh | conformance harness | context | stage |\n| sources/employees.csv | asset | context | stage |\n| sources/sensors.csv | asset | context | stage |\n| sources/orders.csv | asset | context | stage |\n| sources/header_only.csv | asset | context | stage |\n| sources/ragged.csv | asset | context | stage |\n\n## Planning Instructions\n\n### Delivery Shape\n\nCsvStat is a single-binary command-line profiler. The inputs are one CSV file path and optional `--column`, `--freq`, and `--top` options. Profile output is CSV on stdout with `\\n` line endings. Diagnostics go to stderr. Exit codes are 0 (success), 1 (unreadable file), 2 (usage), and 3 (rejected input). The application root is a Go module (`go.mod`, go 1.22 or newer) whose main package lives at `cmd/csvstat/`. Module packages below the root are Plan's choice. There is no persistence and no network I/O.\n\n### Story Realization Map\n\n- **INPUT-001**: scope `cmd/csvstat/` plus a profiling package. every column of a well-formed CSV file | Stdout is the header `column,type,rows,nulls,distinct,min,max,sum,mean,median`, then one row per column in file order. A quoted field containing a comma is read as one value (RFC 4180 via `encoding/csv`). |\n| INPUT-002 | Trim header names and values | Leading and trailing spaces are removed from every header name and value before null, type, distinct, and output rules apply. |\n| INPUT-003 | Reject empty files, malformed headers, and ragged records | An empty file, an empty or repeated header name, or a record whose field count differs from the header exits 3 with nothing on stdout and one stderr line naming the file and line number. |\n\n### Feature: Profile Counts\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| COUNTS-001 | Report rows, nulls, and distinct counts per column | `rows` counts data records. `nulls` counts values that are empty or equal `NA`, `N/A`, or `NULL` ignoring case. `distinct` counts distinct non-null values compared as exact trimmed strings. |\n\n### Feature: Column Types\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| TYPES-001 | Infer integer and number column types | `integer` when every non-null value matches `-?[0-9]+`. `number` when every non-null value matches `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?` and the column is not integer. |\n| TYPES-002 | Infer date column types and reject invalid calendar dates | `date` when every non-null value matches `YYYY-MM-DD` and is a real calendar date. A value such as `2025-02-30` makes the column not date. |\n| TYPES-003 | Infer boolean, text, and empty column types | `boolean` when every non-null value equals `true` or `false` ignoring case. `text` when no earlier type fits. `empty` when the column has no non-null value. |\n\n### Feature: Column Statistics\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| STATS-001 | Report min and max for every column type | `integer` as integers. `number` as two-decimal values. `date` as earliest and latest. `boolean` as `false` if any value is false, else `true`, and `true` if any value is true, else `false`. `text` as smallest and largest by byte value. `empty` blank. |\n| STATS-002 | Report sum and mean for integer and number columns | `sum` is the total of non-null values. `mean` is the total divided by the non-null count. Other types leave both blank. |\n| STATS-003 | Report median for integer and number columns | `median` is the middle sorted non-null value, or the mean of the two middle values when the count is even. Other types leave it blank. |\n| STATS-004 | Write numeric statistics with two decimal places | Every `number` min, max, sum, mean, and median, and every integer mean and median, is written with exactly two decimals, rounded half away from zero, with a leading `-` when negative. Integer min, max, and sum are plain integers. |\n\n### Feature: Column Selection\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| SELECT-001 | Profile one named column with `--column` | Output is the profile header and one row for the named column only. |\n| SELECT-002 | Reject a named column that is absent from the header | `--column` or `--freq` naming a column not in the header exits 3 with nothing on stdout. |\n\n### Feature: Frequency Listing\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| FREQ-001 | List the most frequent values with `--freq` | Header `value,count`, then distinct non-null values ordered by count descending, then value ascending by byte value. Null values are not listed. An all-null column writes only the header. |\n| FREQ-002 | Limit frequency output with `--top` | `--top` defaults to 5 and accepts a positive integer only. `--top` without `--freq` is a usage error. |\n\n### Feature: Command Line Interface\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| CLI-001 | Accept `csvstat [--column <name>] <file>` and `csvstat --freq <name> [--top <n>] <file>` | Options precede exactly one file. `go build ./cmd/csvstat` produces the executable using only the standard library. |\n| CLI-002 | Map usage errors to exit code 2 | A missing or extra file, an unknown option, a missing option value, an invalid `--top`, `--top` without `--freq`, or `--column` with `--freq` exits 2 with nothing on stdout. |\n| CLI-003 | Map an unreadable file to exit code 1 | A file that cannot be opened or read exits 1 with nothing on stdout. |\n\n### Feature: Verification\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| QA-001 | Provide a POSIX `bin/test.sh` | `bin/test.sh` is POSIX sh, passes `sh -n`, runs `go vet ./...` and `go test ./...` from the application root, and exits zero only when both pass. |\n| QA-002 | Pass the supplied grader end to end | `sh sources/run.sh` exits zero against the completed application, and every supplied check passes with zero failures. |\n\n## Surfaced Acceptance Criteria\n\n| ID | Story ID | Criterion |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC-001 | CLI-001 | `csvstat --help` writes usage text naming both command forms and every option to standard output and exits 0. |\n| AC-002 | SELECT-001 | The `--column` and `--freq` name arguments match a trimmed header name exactly and case-sensitively. |\n| AC-003 | STATS-002 | Integer sum and mean are computed with arbitrary precision so that no digit string overflows. |\n| AC-004 | STATS-004 | Number sums, means, and medians are computed from exact decimal values, not binary floating point, and rounded half away from zero only at output. |\n| AC-005 | INPUT-003 | A CSV syntax error reported by `encoding/csv`, such as a bare quote in an unquoted field, exits 3 with nothing on stdout and a stderr line naming the file and line number. |\n| AC-006 | CLI-003 | A path that is a directory, or any path that cannot be opened or read, exits 1 with nothing on stdout. |\n| AC-007 | INPUT-001 | Input bytes are compared as bytes. A byte-order mark is not stripped, and the file is not rejected for invalid UTF-8. |\n\n## Relationship Model\n\n| Source or group | Relationship type | Related source or group | Evidence | Delivery implication |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md | instruction-to-test | sources/run.sh | \"Run Harness\" section names the 22-check grader and its build command. | The grader must be staged verbatim, and the executable must build at `./cmd/csvstat`. |\n| sources/run.sh | test-kit-to-implementation | sources/employees.csv, sources/sensors.csv, sources/orders.csv, sources/header_only.csv, sources/ragged.csv | `DATA` resolves to the directory holding these files (checks A1–A4, E1–E3). | All five fixtures must be staged beside `run.sh` under `sources/`. |\n| sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md | instruction-to-test | sources/employees.csv, sources/sensors.csv, sources/orders.csv, sources/header_only.csv, sources/ragged.csv | \"Read-only scoring assets\" lists each fixture and its shape. | Fixtures are read-only; no story may rewrite them. |\n| sources/run.sh | test-kit-to-implementation | `bin/test.sh` | INSTRUCTIONS \"Tests\" requires `bin/test.sh` to run `go vet` and `go test`. | QA-001 must provide the test runner that the terminal story depends on. |\n\n## Source Roles\n\n| Path | Role | Plan disposition | Build disposition |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md | normative specification | context | prompt-only |\n| sources/run.sh | conformance harness | context | stage |\n| sources/employees.csv | asset | context | stage |\n| sources/sensors.csv | asset | context | stage |\n| sources/orders.csv | asset | context | stage |\n| sources/header_only.csv | asset | context | stage |\n| sources/ragged.csv | asset | context | stage |\n\n## Planning Instructions\n\n### Delivery Shape\n\nCsvStat is a single-binary command-line profiler. The inputs are one CSV file path and optional `--column`, `--freq`, and `--top` options. Profile output is CSV on stdout with `\\n` line endings. Diagnostics go to stderr. Exit codes are 0 (success), 1 (unreadable file), 2 (usage), and 3 (rejected input). The application root is a Go module (`go.mod`, go 1.22 or newer) whose main package lives at `cmd/csvstat/`. Module packages below the root are Plan's choice. There is no persistence and no network I/O.\n\n### Story Realization Map\n\n- **INPUT-001**: scope `cmd/csvstat/` plus a profiling package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Reading the file\", \"Profile output\", \"Output format\". Related: `sources/run.sh` A1–A4. Requires capability and acceptance contract.\n- **INPUT-002**: scope the reader package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Reading the file\" item 2. Related: `sources/orders.csv`, `sources/sensors.csv` (padded and blank values). Requires capability.\n- **INPUT-003**: scope the reader package and CLI exit mapping. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Reading the file\" item 3, \"Exit codes\" 3. Related: `sources/ragged.csv`, `sources/run.sh` E1. Requires capability and acceptance contract.\n- **COUNTS-001**: scope the profiling package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Reading the file\" item 4, \"Profile output\" rows/nulls/distinct. Related: `sources/employees.csv` notes column. Requires capability.\n- **TYPES-001**: scope the type-inference package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Types\" integer and number rows. Related: `sources/sensors.csv` temp_c. Requires capability.\n- **TYPES-002**: scope the type-inference package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Types\" date row. Related: `sources/employees.csv` hire_date, `sources/orders.csv` shipped (`2025-02-30`). Requires capability.\n- **TYPES-003**: scope the type-inference package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Types\" boolean, text, and empty rows. Related: `sources/sensors.csv` battery (all null, type empty). Requires capability.\n- **STATS-001**: scope the statistics package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Profile output\" min/max row. Related: `sources/run.sh` A1–A4. Requires capability.\n- **STATS-002**: scope the statistics package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Profile output\" sum/mean rows. Related: `sources/run.sh` A1–A3 sum and mean. Requires capability.\n- **STATS-003**: scope the statistics package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Profile output\" median row. Related: `sources/run.sh` A1–A3 median (even and odd counts). Requires capability.\n- **STATS-004**: scope the number formatting package. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Decimals\". Related: `sources/run.sh` A1 (`1012.50`), A3 (`3.33`). Requires capability.\n- **SELECT-001**: scope the CLI and profile output. Related: `sources/run.sh` U1–U6. Requires capability and acceptance contract.\n- **CLI-003**: scope the CLI exit mapping. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Exit codes\" 1. Related: `sources/run.sh` E4. Requires test harness.\n- **QA-002**: scope the verification run of `sources/run.sh`. Evidence: INSTRUCTIONS \"Run Harness\". Related: every fixture and every implementation story. Requires acceptance contract and test harness.\n\n### Test and Acceptance Strategy\n\nFocused story tests are `go test` cases written with the story. Each feature story runs only its slice with `go test -run <pattern> ./...` from the application root and declares `Suite: scoped`.\n\nQA-002 is the single terminal verification story. It depends on every other story, runs `sh sources/run.sh` unfiltered, and declares `Suite: full`. Its acceptance is that every supplied check passes with zero failures. `run.sh` exposes no scope selector, so no other story executes it.\n\nQA-001 proves `bin/test.sh` by `sh -n` and by reading its `go vet` and `go test` calls. It does not run the full suite. `run.sh` needs `cmp`, `diff`, `mktemp`, and `sed` on the path.\n\nProgram acceptance is finite and story-scoped. The project-level criteria in SEA_TRIALS.md carry the release gate.\n\n### Sequencing and Dependencies\n\n- CLI-001 has no dependency and comes first. INPUT-001 depends on CLI-001 for invocation, so the two form the first slice.\n- INPUT-002 and INPUT-003 depend on INPUT-001.\n- COUNTS-001 depends on INPUT-002.\n- TYPES-001, TYPES-002, and TYPES-003 depend on COUNTS-001.\n- STATS-001 depends on all three TYPES stories.\n- STATS-002 and STATS-003 depend on TYPES-001 and STATS-001.\n- STATS-004 depends on STATS-002 and STATS-003.\n- SELECT-001 depends on CLI-001 and INPUT-001. SELECT-002 depends on SELECT-001 and INPUT-003.\n- FREQ-001 depends on COUNTS-001 and CLI-001. FREQ-002 depends on FREQ-001 and CLI-002.\n- CLI-002 and CLI-003 depend on CLI-001.\n- QA-001 depends on INPUT-001.\n- QA-002 depends on QA-001 and every implementation story.\n- External dependencies: Go 1.22 or newer. No network or database.\n\n### Source Conflicts and Gaps\n\n**Cross-source conflicts: none.** `INSTRUCTIONS.md`, `sources/run.sh` known answers, and the prior `SEA_TRIALS.md` agree. The known answers were spot-checked against the fixtures (employees id sum/mean/median, manager_id, notes distinct and max, sensors reading_id sum and battery, orders order_id sum, qty sum and median, customer frequencies, sensor frequency ties) and agree.\n\n**Gaps resolved by the narrowest behavior, not by questionnaire.** These are persisted in Surfaced Acceptance Criteria:\n\n1. A CSV syntax error from `encoding/csv` exits 3 (AC-005). The sources list only ragged records under exit 3, so this is the nearest rejection code.\n2. `--column` and `--freq` names match trimmed header names exactly and case-sensitively (AC-002).\n3. Integer sums and means use arbitrary precision (AC-003).\n4. Number arithmetic is exact decimal, rounded only at output (AC-004).\n5. `--help` writes to stdout and exits 0 (AC-001). This is the CLI help-text baseline.\n6. A directory or unopenable path exits 1 (AC-006).\n7. Bytes are compared as bytes. No BOM stripping and no UTF-8 rejection (AC-007).\n\nNo measurement facts are missing. The sources contain no numeric release threshold, so no `## Questions` entries are needed.\n\n## Analysis Notes\n\ngenerated: 2026-10-24\nblueprint: /mnt/c/Users/barlo/projects/drydock/uat/CsvStat/runs/20261025.001433/workspace/targets/CsvStat/blueprint\n\nQuality: Ready\n  blockers: 0\n  questions: 0\n  features: 8\n  stories: 20\n  stack: Go 1.22 or newer, standard library only\n  display_name: not proposed\n  short_description: not proposed\n\nProject type: cli. automated test pass.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: ubiquitous\nCommand: [\"sh\", \"bin/test.sh\"]\n\n## st-003: The executable builds with go build\n\nType: technical\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: The application root shall be a Go module from which go build ./cmd/csvstat produces the csvstat executable using only the standard library.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: ubiquitous\nCommand: [\"go\", \"build\", \"-o\", \"csvstat\", \"./cmd/csvstat\"]\n\n## st-004: Column types are inferred\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: The application shall assign each column the first of integer,
